<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove canonical nonvanishing for smooth connected projective complex fourfolds: if <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is pseudo-effective, then $`H^0(X,mK_X)\ne0`$ for some positive integer <i>m</i>.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-nonvanishing)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex fourfold. If $K_X$ is pseudo-effective, then $H^0(X,mK_X)\ne0$ for some positive integer $m$.
  - Explanation: Obtains a pluricanonical section under pseudo-effectivity.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lc-nonvanishing)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a connected normal projective log canonical complex pair of dimension at most four. Suppose that $\Delta\geq0$ is rational and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef. For every positive integer $r$ such that $rD$ is Cartier, there is a positive integer $m$ with $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mrD))\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Produces a section on the original log canonical pair with a prescribed Cartier index.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-reduction)): If [thm:nonvanishing](#thm:nonvanishing) fails, there is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold $Y$, with $K=K_Y$, such that* 1. *$K$ is nef, $\kappa(Y,K)=-\infty$, and $K^4=0$;* 2. *$q(Y)=0$ and the nef dimension of $K$ is four;* 3. *for some integer $\iota>0$, $\iota K$ is Cartier and $$\label{eq:curve-lower-bound} K\cdot C\ge \frac1\iota$$ for every integral curve $C$ through a point of a fixed very general locus in $Y$.
  - Explanation: Fixes the geometry of a hypothetical terminal counterexample.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-subvarieties)): For $Y$ as in , every proper positive-dimensional subvariety through a point of a suitable fixed very general locus is of general type.
  - Explanation: Forces general type for proper subvarieties through the very general locus.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-numerical-subadjunction)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety over $\mathbb C$, let $\Theta\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety. Suppose that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $Z$, and let $\rho:V^*\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then $$\label{eq:numerical-subadjunction} K_{V^*}\cdot(\rho^*P)^{f-1} \leq (K_Z+\Theta)\|_V\cdot(P\|_V)^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection of the restricted rational Cartier class with the cycle $[V]$. No $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.
  - Explanation: Bounds canonical intersections at a generic log canonical center.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-base-component)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\ne\mathcal H\subset H^0(Z,\mathcal O_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\eta_V$, and that $\eta_V\in Z_{\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\mathfrak m=\mathfrak m_{\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}}$. If $$\mathfrak b\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}\subset\mathfrak m^h \qquad(h\in\mathbb Z_{>0}),$$ then $$\label{eq:base-component-degree} P^f\cdot V\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ If $f>0$, there is a rational number $c$ with $0<c\leq ak/h$ such that, on a smooth resolution $V^*$, $$\label{eq:base-component-canonical} K_{V^*}\cdot P^{f-1} \leq (K_Z+cP)\|_V\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The coefficient $c$ may be chosen with an effective rational divisor $\Theta\sim_{\mathbb Q}cP$ such that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. Thus can also be applied with a different nef testing class. The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the degree and canonical intersection of a high-multiplicity base component.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-moving-multiplicity)): Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\tau_0<\tau_1<\cdots<\tau_d, \qquad\tau_{i+1}-\tau_i=\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\ceil{k\tau_i}$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\delta\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\Gamma=\{(t,z):z\in V_t\}\subset T\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\label{eq:moving-multiplicity-order} \mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\qquad h=\ceil{k\delta/2}, \qquad k/h\leq2/\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.* *More precisely, the incidence base ideal lies in $\mathcal I_\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\Gamma$. If a further morphism $Z\to B$ is given, one may choose a general incidence point where the base support is only $V_t$ and $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its image in $B$. For the fiber component $F$ through that point, assume that $F$ is proper in the integral ambient fiber component under consideration. The restricted series is then nonzero and has base ideal in the ordinary $\mathcal I_F^h$ and has $F$ as an isolated base component there. This last assertion concerns the scheme fiber locally at that chosen point, and does not assert transversality of every fiber. Smoothness near the generic point of $F$, and the projectivity and klt hypotheses needed for on the ambient fiber, must be checked in each application.
  - Explanation: Controls ordinary ideal powers by differentiating the full moving jet kernels.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-obstruction)): Keep $Y$, $L_t$, and $\iota$ as in [eq:small-volume-polarization](#eq:small-volume-polarization), [eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound](#eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound). Fix $f\geq1$ and positive constants $C_0,C_1$. Suppose, for arbitrarily small $t$, that there are a smooth projective variety $V_t^*$ of dimension $f$, a reduced simple normal crossings divisor $G_t$ on it, a nef rational divisor $P_t$, and a morphism $e_t:V_t^*\to Y$ satisfying the following conditions:* 1. *$K_{V_t^*}+G_t$ is big;* 2. *$0<P_t^f\leq C_0$ and $(K_{V_t^*}+G_t)\cdot P_t^{f-1}\leq C_1P_t^f$;* 3. *$e_t$ is generically finite onto its positive-dimensional image, and that image meets the fixed very general locus of $Y$;* 4. *$P_t-e_t^*L_t$ is nef.* *Then these conditions are impossible for all sufficiently small $t$. The case $G_t=0$ includes ordinary general type.
  - Explanation: Excludes bounded-degree logarithmic general-type data as the polarization varies.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-orders)): For all sufficiently small positive rational $t$, a very general point $x\in Y_{\mathrm{sm}}$ has the following property. For every positive integer $k$ for which $kP_{\mathrm s}$ is Cartier and every nonzero section $s\in H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y(kP_{\mathrm s}))$, one has $$\label{eq:scalar-orders} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)\le4k\rho_t\le32k\epsilon .$$
  - Explanation: Bounds all allowed scalar section orders at very general points.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-adjoint-metric)): Let $(H,\Theta)$ be a normal connected projective complex klt pair, where $\Theta\ge0$ is rational and $D_H=K_H+\Theta$ is nef and $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Let $\pi:V\to H$ be any projective log resolution. The rational line bundle $\pi^*D_H$ admits a semipositive metric with minimal singularities, and every such metric has zero Lelong number at every point of $V$. Its multiplier ideal is trivial for every positive exponent. Neither $K_H$ nor $\Theta$ is required to be separately $\mathbb Q$-Cartier.
  - Explanation: Provides zero-Lelong minimal metrics for actual nef klt adjoint bundles.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-interior-injectivity)): Let $V$ be a smooth projective complex variety and $L$ an integral divisor. Let $0\le C_0\le C_2$ be effective rational divisors whose combined support has simple normal crossings. Suppose the actual rational line bundles $L-C_0$ and $L-C_2$ have semipositive singular Hermitian metrics with zero Lelong numbers at every point. For a rational number $0<\lambda<1$ put $C_1=(1-\lambda)C_0+\lambda C_2$. The natural inclusion of sheaves induces an injection $$H^1(V,\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\lfloor C_1\rfloor)) \longrightarrow H^1(V,\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\lfloor C_0\rfloor)).$$
  - Explanation: Injects ordinary cohomology for an interior boundary into the endpoint group.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-alternative)): Let $Y$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold such that $K_Y$ is nef and $\kappa(Y,K_Y)=-\infty$. Suppose that there is a very general locus of $Y$ through whose points every proper positive-dimensional subvariety is of general type on resolution. Let $J$ be a rational Weil divisor, with coefficients of either sign, satisfying $J\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_Y$. Let $p:W\to Y$ be a projective log resolution, and let $D_W$ be a reduced simple normal crossings divisor containing the strict support of $J$ and every $p$-exceptional divisor. Then $K_W+D_W$ is big.
  - Explanation: Makes the resolved full support of a signed canonical representative logarithmically big.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-slot-ambient)): The varieties $Y\times Y$, $Z$, and $Z_{\mathrm{sl}}$ are projective, $\mathbb Q$-factorial, and klt. The tautological classes of the two bundles are nef, and $P$ and $P_{\mathrm{sl}}$ are ample rational divisors. Writing $K_\Sigma=K_1+K_2$ on the product and $K_\Sigma=K$ on a slice, one has $$\label{eq:two-slot-canonical} K_{\mathrm{bundle}} =-2\xi+(\iota+1)K_\Sigma \le\frac{\iota+1}{r_t}\,P_{\mathrm{bundle}}$$ in nef order.
  - Explanation: Establishes the ambient singularity and positivity properties of the bundles.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-slot-volumes)): Assume $q\iota/r_t\le1$. For sufficiently small $t$, there are positive constants $c_n,C_n$, depending only on $n$, such that $$\label{eq:two-slot-total-volume} c_n q\epsilon^{2n}\le P^d\le C_n q\epsilon^{2n}.$$ On either slice, $$\label{eq:two-slot-slice-volume} 0<P_{\mathrm{sl}}^{\,n+1} \le2^{n+2}q\epsilon^n .$$
  - Explanation: Bounds bundle volumes using the split projective-bundle formula.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-slot-jets)): Let $Z$ and $P$ be defined by . Fix $\epsilon>0$, choose $\delta$ satisfying [eq:two-slot-gap-choice](#eq:two-slot-gap-choice), and then fix $q$ large enough for [eq:two-slot-jet-volume](#eq:two-slot-jet-volume). For all sufficiently small positive rational $t$, one has $$\label{eq:two-slot-seshadri} \varepsilon(P;z)>2n+2=10$$ at a very general smooth point of the torus open of $Z$.
  - Explanation: Obtains a strict local positivity bound at very general torus points.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-slot-correspondences)): Fix $\epsilon>0$, an integer $q>0$, and positive constants $B_0,B_1$. Keep $Y,K,A,L_t$ and $P=L_1+L_2+q\xi$ as above. For all sufficiently small positive rational $t$, there is no irreducible finite-type parameter space $T$ and integral closed subvariety $\Gamma\subset T\times Z$, dominant over $T$, with the following properties:* 1. *The evaluation $\Gamma\to Z$ is dominant, and a general fiber $V$ over $T$ is an integral subvariety of dimension $n$.* 2. *On a smooth projective resolution $V^*\to V$, both projections $g_i:V^*\to Y$, $i=1,2$, are dominant and generically finite.* 3. *Writing $P$ also for its pullback to $V^*$, one has $$0<P^n\cdot V\le B_0,\qquad K_{V^*}P^{n-1}\le B_1(P^n\cdot V).$$* *The restriction on $t$ depends only on the fixed geometry and $\epsilon,q,B_0,B_1$, not on the parameter space or family.
  - Explanation: Excludes sweeping families with bounded degree and canonical intersection.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fixed-two-slot-jets)): Fix sufficiently small $t$ as in , and let $p:W\to Y$ be any fixed smooth projective resolution. Continue to denote pullbacks by $L$ and $S_0$. On $$Z_W=\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal O_W(S_0)_1\oplus\mathcal O_W(S_0)_2\bigr) \longrightarrow W\times W,\qquad M=L_1+L_2+q\xi ,$$ there are a smooth torus point $z_*$, over the isomorphism opens of $p$ in both slots, and a sufficiently divisible integer $k_0>0$ such that $k_0L$ is Cartier and $$\label{eq:fixed-two-slot-jet-map} H^0(Z_W,\mathcal O_{Z_W}(k_0M)) \longrightarrow \mathcal O_{Z_W}(k_0M)\otimes \bigl(\mathcal O_{Z_W,z_*}/\mathfrak m_{z_*}^{\,10k_0+1}\bigr)$$ is surjective. In particular, finitely many sections can be fixed whose jets span its target.
  - Explanation: Fixes a finite surjective jet system on a smooth model.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-data)): Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\ge2$. Fix rational Cartier divisors $L,H$, with $H$ ample, an integral Cartier divisor $S$, an integer $q>0$, and real numbers $r>1$, $\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\begin{gathered} H^n\le(2\epsilon)^n,\qquad K_WH^{n-1}\le 2H^n/r,\qquad (2L+qS)H^{n-1}\le4H^n,\\ (14\epsilon)^n<1/4,\qquad80\epsilon<3/4. \end{gathered}$$ The following three kinds of fixed data cannot all exist.* 1. *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag ending in a curve $C$, of successive divisor classes $l_iH$ for positive integers $l_i$, with $\mu_{\min}(\Omega_W^1\|_C)\ge0$.* 2. *On $Z_W=\mathbb P(\mathcal O(S)_1\oplus\mathcal O(S)_2)\to W\times W$, in the symmetric-power convention, put $M=L_1+L_2+q\xi$. For some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0L$ Cartier, finitely many sections of $k_0M$ surject onto $\mathcal O_{Z_W,z_*}/\mathfrak m_{z_*}^{(2n+2)k_0+1}$ after trivialization at a smooth torus point $z_*$.* 3. *A point $x\in W$, the blowup $\pi_x:\widehat W\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J_x$, and a curve class $\gamma=f_*(A_1\cdots A_{n-1})$, where $f:V\to\widehat W$ is a fixed birational morphism from a smooth integral projective variety and the $A_i$ are ample Cartier divisors, such that $J_x\gamma>0$ and $$\pi_x^*(L+K_W/2+\lambda S)\gamma \le40\epsilon J_x\gamma\qquad(0\le\lambda\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoint inequalities.* *No nefness of $L,S,K_W$ is assumed, and $z_*$ need not lie over $x$. All divisors, the flag, the point, the curve representative, and the finite jet sections are fixed over $\mathbb C$ before the residual characteristic varies.
  - Explanation: Excludes simultaneous existence of the prescribed geometric and numerical data.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-movable-test)): There is a strongly movable curve class $\gamma$ on $\widehat W$, represented as the pushforward of an ample complete intersection on a fixed smooth projective birational model, such that $$\label{eq:movable-test} (\pi_x^*D^+)\cdot\gamma <40\epsilon\,J_x\cdot\gamma,\qquad J_x\cdot\gamma>0.$$
  - Explanation: Produces an actual strongly movable complete-intersection test class.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-feasibility)): Let $M=(m_{ij})\in\operatorname{Mat}_{w\times v}(\mathbb Q)$ and $d\in\mathbb Q^w$. If the system $$\label{eq:rational-feasibility} d_i-y_i=\sum_{j=1}^{v}m_{ij}b_j\quad(1\le i\le w), \qquad y_i\ge0$$ has a real solution, it has a rational solution. Moreover, one may require precisely the same $y_i$ to be zero as in the given solution. Consequently, if $D$ is a rational Weil divisor on a normal integral variety $X$ over a field $k$ and $D\sim_{\mathbb R}G$ for an effective real Weil divisor $G$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}G_{\mathbb Q}$ for an effective rational divisor with $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G_{\mathbb Q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G$. If $rD$ is Cartier for a prescribed positive integer $r$, then $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mrD))\ne0$ for some $m>0$.
  - Explanation: Converts real feasibility to rational feasibility while preserving the vanishing coordinates.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-moving-multiplicity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\ceil{k\\tau_i}$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{eq:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\ceil{k\\delta/2},\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*More precisely, the incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If a further morphism $Z\\to B$ is given, one may choose a general incidence point where the base support is only $V_t$ and $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its image in $B$. For the fiber component $F$ through that point, assume that $F$ is proper in the integral ambient fiber component under consideration. The restricted series is then nonzero and has base ideal in the ordinary $\\mathcal I_F^h$ and has $F$ as an isolated base component there. This last assertion concerns the scheme fiber locally at that chosen point, and does not assert transversality of every fiber. Smoothness near the generic point of $F$, and the projectivity and klt hypotheses needed for on the ambient fiber, must be checked in each application.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-obstruction",
        "exact_quote": "Keep $Y$, $L_t$, and $\\iota$ as in [eq:small-volume-polarization](#eq:small-volume-polarization), [eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound](#eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound). Fix $f\\geq1$ and positive constants $C_0,C_1$. Suppose, for arbitrarily small $t$, that there are a smooth projective variety $V_t^*$ of dimension $f$, a reduced simple normal crossings divisor $G_t$ on it, a nef rational divisor $P_t$, and a morphism $e_t:V_t^*\\to Y$ satisfying the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *$K_{V_t^*}+G_t$ is big;*\n\n2.  *$0<P_t^f\\leq C_0$ and $(K_{V_t^*}+G_t)\\cdot P_t^{f-1}\\leq C_1P_t^f$;*\n\n3.  *$e_t$ is generically finite onto its positive-dimensional image, and that image meets the fixed very general locus of $Y$;*\n\n4.  *$P_t-e_t^*L_t$ is nef.*\n\n*Then these conditions are impossible for all sufficiently small $t$. The case $G_t=0$ includes ordinary general type.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-orders",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small positive rational $t$, a very general point $x\\in Y_{\\mathrm{sm}}$ has the following property. For every positive integer $k$ for which $kP_{\\mathrm s}$ is Cartier and every nonzero section $s\\in H^0(Y,\\mathcal O_Y(kP_{\\mathrm s}))$, one has $$\\label{eq:scalar-orders}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)\\le4k\\rho_t\\le32k\\epsilon .$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-034.6"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-adjoint-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(H,\\Theta)$ be a normal connected projective complex klt pair, where $\\Theta\\ge0$ is rational and $D_H=K_H+\\Theta$ is nef and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Let $\\pi:V\\to H$ be any projective log resolution. The rational line bundle $\\pi^*D_H$ admits a semipositive metric with minimal singularities, and every such metric has zero Lelong number at every point of $V$. Its multiplier ideal is trivial for every positive exponent. Neither $K_H$ nor $\\Theta$ is required to be separately $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-adjoint-metric",

          "source_id": "PM-034.6"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-interior-injectivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a smooth projective complex variety and $L$ an integral divisor. Let $0\\le C_0\\le C_2$ be effective rational divisors whose combined support has simple normal crossings. Suppose the actual rational line bundles $L-C_0$ and $L-C_2$ have semipositive singular Hermitian metrics with zero Lelong numbers at every point. For a rational number $0<\\lambda<1$ put $C_1=(1-\\lambda)C_0+\\lambda C_2$. The natural inclusion of sheaves induces an injection $$H^1(V,\\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\\lfloor C_1\\rfloor))\n \\longrightarrow H^1(V,\\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\\lfloor C_0\\rfloor)).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-interior-injectivity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a smooth projective complex variety and $L$ an integral divisor. Suppose the actual rational line bundles $L-C_0$ and $L-C_2$ have semipositive singular Hermitian metrics with zero Lelong numbers at every point. For a rational number $0<\\lambda<1$ put $C_1=(1-\\lambda)C_0+\\lambda C_2$. The natural inclusion of sheaves induces an injection $$H^1(V,\\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\\lfloor C_1\\rfloor))\n \\longrightarrow H^1(V,\\mathcal O_V(K_V+L-\\lfloor C_0\\rfloor)).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be a smooth projective complex variety and $L$ an integral divisor. Let $0\\le C_0\\le C_2$ be effective rational divisors whose combined support has simple normal crossings. Suppose the actual rational line bundles $L-C_0$ and $L-C_2$ have semipositive singular Hermitian metrics with zero Lelong numbers at every point.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-alternative",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold such that $K_Y$ is nef and $\\kappa(Y,K_Y)=-\\infty$. Suppose that there is a very general locus of $Y$ through whose points every proper positive-dimensional subvariety is of general type on resolution. Let $J$ be a rational Weil divisor, with coefficients of either sign, satisfying $J\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}K_Y$. Let $p:W\\to Y$ be a projective log resolution, and let $D_W$ be a reduced simple normal crossings divisor containing the strict support of $J$ and every $p$-exceptional divisor. Then $K_W+D_W$ is big.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-alternative",

Then $K_W+D_W$ is big.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.6"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-slot-ambient",
        "exact_quote": "The varieties $Y\\times Y$, $Z$, and $Z_{\\mathrm{sl}}$ are projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, and klt. The tautological classes of the two bundles are nef, and $P$ and $P_{\\mathrm{sl}}$ are ample rational divisors. Writing $K_\\Sigma=K_1+K_2$ on the product and $K_\\Sigma=K$ on a slice, one has $$\\label{eq:two-slot-canonical}\n K_{\\mathrm{bundle}}\n =-2\\xi+(\\iota+1)K_\\Sigma\n \\le\\frac{\\iota+1}{r_t}\\,P_{\\mathrm{bundle}}$$ in nef order.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-slot-ambient",
          "exact_quote": "The varieties $Y\\times Y$, $Z$, and $Z_{\\mathrm{sl}}$ are projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, and klt.           "source_id": "PM-034.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The varieties $Y\\times Y$, $Z$, and $Z_{\\mathrm{sl}}$ are projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, and klt.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-slot-volumes",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $q\\iota/r_t\\le1$.         "source_id": "PM-034.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-034.6"

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\epsilon>0$, an integer $q>0$, and positive constants $B_0,B_1$. Keep $Y,K,A,L_t$ and $P=L_1+L_2+q\\xi$ as above. Suppose $$\\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le 2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2L+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<1/4,\\qquad80\\epsilon<3/4.\n\\end{gathered}$$ The following three kinds of fixed data cannot all exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag ending in a curve $C$, of successive divisor classes $l_iH$ for positive integers $l_i$, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On $Z_W=\\mathbb P(\\mathcal O(S)_1\\oplus\\mathcal O(S)_2)\\to W\\times W$, in the symmetric-power convention, put $M=L_1+L_2+q\\xi$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pseudo-effectivity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Pseudo-effectivity says that the numerical class is a limit of effective divisor classes.
- **Very general locus** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A very general locus is the complement of a countable union of proper closed subsets.
- **General type of a subvariety** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A subvariety is of general type when a smooth projective resolution is of general type.
- **Minimal singularities** ([source](#sec:analytic-inputs)): A semipositive singular Hermitian metric has *minimal singularities* if its local weight is, up to a bounded additive error, no more singular than the weight of any other semipositive metric on the same bundle.

| Q7 | Degree to multiplicity ratio | ≤ | $2/\delta$ | Positive gap and allowed integral section degree |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reduction): Minimal model reduction -> A fixed terminal canonical model
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-orders): Differentiation of full jet kernels and degree obstruction -> Uniform scalar vanishing bounds
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-alternative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-alternative): Interior injectivity and whole-floor abundance -> Logarithmic bigness of the full resolved support
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-slot-correspondences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-slot-correspondences): Branch control and finite-cover comparison -> Exclusion of dominant finite correspondences
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-cor-fixed-two-slot-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fixed-two-slot-jets): Seshadri positivity and Serre vanishing -> One finite surjective jet system
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-movable-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-movable-test): Movable-curve duality -> A fixed complete-intersection curve witness
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-nonvanishing) / [proof](#pm-h-41): Finite-data Frobenius rank and determinant-order comparison -> Canonical nonvanishing
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-feasibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-feasibility): Rational Gaussian elimination and approximation -> Rational coefficients with the same effective support

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **canonical-nonvanishing**: Pseudo-effectivity of the canonical divisor gives a nonzero pluricanonical section.
- **rational-support**: Rational feasibility preserves exactly the vanishing coordinates of a given real solution.
- **qualitative-section**: The existence conclusion supplies no uniform bound on the section degree.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **uniform-degree**: Treat nonvanishing as a bounded search for a section. -> A finite search may terminate without finding the existential section.
- **support-loss**: Replace support preservation by nonnegativity alone. -> A rational solution can lose a required positive coordinate.
- **numerical-metric**: Apply the analytic lifting inputs using numerical divisor classes alone. -> Numerical data do not provide the required line bundles and sheaf maps.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite rational feasibility is constructive through Gaussian elimination and rational approximation preserving support. The executable contract covers finite rational systems; it does not construct the geometric pluricanonical section.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of rational M, d, and prescribed zero-coordinate indices.
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty rectangular M; at most three rows and columns; matching d; valid zero indices.
# OUTPUTS: Rational b,y witness with exactly the prescribed zeros, or unsat.
# TIME: Bounded exact linear-real solver call per case; no polynomial bound asserted.
# SPACE: Solver-dependent for the finite rational constraint system.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational linear real arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid input, unknown solver result, or timeout raises an error.
import json, sys
from fractions import Fraction
from z3 import Solver, Real, RealVal, sat, unsat

def solve(c):
    M, d, zero = c['M'], c['d'], set(c['zero'])
    w, v = len(M), len(M[0])
    assert 1 <= w <= 3 and 1 <= v <= 3
    assert len(d) == w and all(len(row) == v for row in M)
    assert all(type(i) is int and 0 <= i < w for i in zero)
    def rational(x):
        f = Fraction(str(x))
        return RealVal(str(f.numerator) + '/' + str(f.denominator))
    b = [Real('b%d' % j) for j in range(v)]
    y = [Real('y%d' % i) for i in range(w)]
    engine = Solver()
    engine.set(timeout=2000)
    for i in range(w):
        engine.add(rational(d[i]) - y[i] == sum(rational(M[i][j])*b[j] for j in range(v)))
        engine.add(y[i] == 0 if i in zero else y[i] > 0)
    answer = engine.check()
    if answer == unsat:
        return {'status': 'unsat'}
    assert answer == sat
    model = engine.model()
    def value(x):
        q = model.eval(x, model_completion=True)
        return str(q.numerator_as_long()) + '/' + str(q.denominator_as_long())
    return {'status': 'sat', 'b': [value(x) for x in b], 'y': [value(x) for x in y]}

cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert 1 <= len(cases) <= 500
print(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in cases]))
```

  "panel3": {

    "oracle_code": "import json, sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Q\n\n# Fourier-Motzkin elimination on inequalities a.b <= rhs, with strictness.\ndef possible(item):\n    rows = []\n    Z = set(item['zero'])\n    for index, coefficients in enumerate(item['M']):\n        a = tuple(Q(str(t)) for t in coefficients)\n        rhs = Q(str(item['d'][index]))\n        rows.append((a, rhs, index not in Z))\n        if index in Z:\n            rows.append((tuple(-t for t in a), -rhs, False))\n    for _ in range(len(item['M'][0])):\n        pos = [t for t in rows if t[0][-1] > 0]\n        neg = [t for t in rows if t[0][-1] < 0]\n        reduced = [(a[:-1], r, s) for a,r,s in rows if a[-1] == 0]\n        for a,r,s in pos:\n            for b,t,u in neg:\n                reduced.append((tuple((-b[-1])*x+a[-1]*z for x,z in zip(a[:-1],b[:-1])), (-b[-1])*r+a[-1]*t, s or u))\n        if len(reduced) > 50000:\n            raise RuntimeError('elimination resource ceiling')\n        rows = reduced\n    return all(r > 0 if strict else r >= 0 for a,r,strict in rows)\n\ntry:\n    assert sys.argv[3] == 'EXACT_FEASIBILITY'\n    inputs = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\n    outputs = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\n    assert len(inputs) == len(outputs)\n    for case, output in zip(inputs, outputs):\n        feasible = possible(case)\n        assert output['status'] == ('sat' if feasible else 'unsat')\n        if feasible:\n            B = [Q(t) for t in output['b']]\n            Y = [Q(t) for t in output['y']]\n            assert len(B) == len(case['M'][0]) and len(Y) == len(case['d'])\n            for i, row in enumerate(case['M']):\n                assert Q(str(case['d'][i])) - Y[i] == sum(Q(str(a))*b for a,b in zip(row,B))\n                assert Y[i] == 0 if i in case['zero'] else Y[i] > 0\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(1)\nsys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "For each case, unsat or rational b,y satisfying the affine equations, with exactly the prescribed zero coordinates and every other coordinate positive.",
    "reason": "Finite rational feasibility is constructive through Gaussian elimination and rational approximation preserving support. The executable contract covers finite rational systems; it does not construct the geometric pluricanonical section.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of rational M, d, and prescribed zero-coordinate indices.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty rectangular M; at most three rows and columns; matching d; valid zero indices.\n# OUTPUTS: Rational b,y witness with exactly the prescribed zeros, or unsat.\n# TIME: Bounded exact linear-real solver call per case; no polynomial bound asserted.\n# SPACE: Solver-dependent for the finite rational constraint system.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational linear real arithmetic; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input, unknown solver result, or timeout raises an error.\nimport json, sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfrom z3 import Solver, Real, RealVal, sat, unsat\n\ndef solve(c):\n    M, d, zero = c['M'], c['d'], set(c['zero'])\n    w, v = len(M), len(M[0])\n    assert 1 <= w <= 3 and 1 <= v <= 3\n    assert len(d) == w and all(len(row) == v for row in M)\n    assert all(type(i) is int and 0 <= i < w for i in zero)\n    def rational(x):\n        f = Fraction(str(x))\n        return RealVal(str(f.numerator) + '/' + str(f.denominator))\n    b = [Real('b%d' % j) for j in range(v)]\n    y = [Real('y%d' % i) for i in range(w)]\n    engine = Solver()\n    engine.set(timeout=2000)\n    for i in range(w):\n        engine.add(rational(d[i]) - y[i] == sum(rational(M[i][j])*b[j] for j in range(v)))\n        engine.add(y[i] == 0 if i in zero else y[i] > 0)\n    answer = engine.check()\n    if answer == unsat:\n        return {'status': 'unsat'}\n    assert answer == sat\n    model = engine.model()\n    def value(x):\n        q = model.eval(x, model_completion=True)\n        return str(q.numerator_as_long()) + '/' + str(q.denominator_as_long())\n    return {'status': 'sat', 'b': [value(x) for x in b], 'y': [value(x) for x in y]}\n\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert 1 <= len(cases) <= 500\nprint(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in cases]))\n",
    "test_vectors": [

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geometric nonvanishing conclusion supplies no uniform section-degree bound; no software implementation contract is stated. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geometric existence result supplies no simulation state evolution or game rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite rational-feasibility proof supplies a mathematical procedure, but no tool interface or operational contract is stated. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite rational-feasibility proof converts a feasible real affine system with rational coefficients into a rational feasible point preserving its zero coordinates. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Rational matrices occur as inputs to feasibility; no data-structure invariant or operation is established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The arguments concern projective geometry and rational feasibility; no accelerator mapping or hardware resource bound is given. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Existence of pluricanonical sections and rational feasible points supplies no hardness or security assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rational-feasibility assertion supplies neither a learning objective nor statistical guarantees. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For rational affine equalities and nonnegative slack variables, rational feasibility preserves the support of a given real feasible point; this supports exact feasible-witness reconstruction, without an objective-value claim. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof supplies no distributed execution, communication, or failure model. |
| other (Algebraic geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Canonical nonvanishing applies to smooth connected projective complex fourfolds with pseudo-effective canonical divisor. The rational log canonical consequence preserves a prescribed Cartier index on the original normal variety. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Canonical nonvanishing is stated for smooth connected projective complex fourfolds with pseudo-effective canonical divisor. Blocked use: ['Applying the smooth theorem to arbitrary dimensions or ground fields without further results.']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The pluricanonical section conclusion is qualitative and supplies no uniform bound on its degree. Blocked use: ['Treating nonvanishing as a bounded section-search procedure.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The log canonical consequence requires an effective rational boundary and a nef rational Cartier adjoint. Blocked use: ['Dropping rationality, log canonicity, or nefness from this stated corollary.']
- **L4** (edge_case): The prescribed Cartier index does not make the section degree uniform across pairs. Blocked use: ['Inferring a common section degree from a common Cartier index.']
- **L5** (counterexample_scope): Nonvanishing alone does not assert semiampleness; the abundance consequence uses additional lifting results. Blocked use: ['Reading the first section as global generation of a multiple.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The analytic metric input is specific to nef klt adjoints. Blocked use: ['Applying its metric regularity conclusion to a general nef line bundle.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): Interior injectivity requires actual rational line bundles with the stated endpoint metrics. Blocked use: ['Replacing rational line-bundle hypotheses by numerical equivalence.']
- **L8** (edge_case): The canonical base-component estimate requires a positive-dimensional component, although the degree estimate also covers a point. Blocked use: ['Using the canonical estimate for a zero-dimensional base component.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The moving multiplicity argument uses entire jet kernels and ordinary local ideal powers. Blocked use: ['Substituting an arbitrary subseries without derivative closure.']
- **L10** (approximation): All geometric witnesses must be fixed over the complex numbers before reduction to positive characteristic. Blocked use: ['Choosing witnesses anew for each residual characteristic.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): A bounded degree alone does not yield a finite list of covers; branch control is required. Blocked use: ['Replacing the fixed branch-complement argument with a numerical degree bound alone.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): Finite rational feasibility uses rational affine coefficients and preserves the zero coordinates of a given real solution. Blocked use: ['Applying this support-preservation assertion to nonlinear constraints or irrational coefficient systems.', 'Inferring an objective optimum or integer feasible point.']
- **L13** (edge_case): The rational-feasibility argument includes vanishing slack coordinates and an affine space with no free directions. Blocked use: ['Discarding a feasible boundary point because some slack coordinates vanish.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove canonical nonvanishing for smooth connected projective complex fourfolds: if <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is pseudo-effective, then $`H^0(X,mK_X)\ne0`$ for some positive integer <i>m</i>.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth projective variety $X$, canonical nonvanishing asks whether pseudo-effectivity of $K_X$ guarantees a pluricanonical form: a nonzero section of $mK_X$ for some integer $m>0$. Pseudo-effectivity says that the numerical class is a limit of effective divisor classes. The question concerns the canonical line bundle itself, rather than an approximation to its numerical class. Over $\mathbb C$, pseudo-effectivity of $K_X$ is equivalent to $X$ not being covered by rational curves <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BDPP2013](#ref-BDPP2013), Corollary 0.3]. Nonvanishing thus asks for a pluricanonical form on every smooth projective variety outside the uniruled class.

Nonvanishing is weaker than abundance. For a nef canonical divisor, abundance asks for *semiampleness*: some positive multiple is generated by its global sections and therefore defines a morphism. Obtaining the first section is a separate difficulty. We prove the following nonvanishing theorem in dimension four.

<a id="stmt-thm-nonvanishing"></a>
<div id="thm:nonvanishing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex fourfold. If $K_X$ is pseudo-effective, then $H^0(X,mK_X)\ne0$ for some positive integer $m$.*

</div>

The theorem imposes no condition on numerical dimension, irregularity, or holomorphic Euler characteristic. Its conclusion is qualitative; it gives no uniform bound on the integer $m$.

The threefold results explain both the history of the problem and the lower-dimensional inputs used here. Miyaoka proved nonvanishing for minimal threefolds <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Miyaoka1988Nonvanishing](#ref-Miyaoka1988Nonvanishing)] and then abundance in numerical dimension one <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Miyaoka1988NuOne](#ref-Miyaoka1988NuOne), pp. 203–204]. Kawamata’s work on pluricanonical systems and his abundance theorem for minimal threefolds established the broader canonical case <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kawamata1985](#ref-Kawamata1985), [Kawamata1992](#ref-Kawamata1992)]. Keel–Matsuki–McKernan proved log abundance for threefolds, with the correction included here <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KMM1994](#ref-KMM1994), [KMM2004](#ref-KMM2004)]. Boundary arguments also require sections to agree where components meet: Fujino’s semi-log-canonical threefold abundance theorem supplies generation on the whole reduced floor, including its conductor identifications <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Fujino2000](#ref-Fujino2000), Corollary 4.10]. These are established lower-dimensional theorems used before the new fourfold argument.

Several important fourfold cases were already known. Fujino proved semiampleness for canonical fourfolds with nef canonical divisor and positive irregularity. He distinguished nonvanishing for non-uniruled fourfolds of irregularity zero from the remaining abundance question in Kodaira dimension zero <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Fujino2010](#ref-Fujino2010), Corollary 4.7 and Problems 4.10–4.11]. Lazić–Peternell proved nonvanishing for $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal projective minimal varieties with canonical numerical dimension one and nonzero holomorphic Euler characteristic <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LazicPeternell2018](#ref-LazicPeternell2018), Theorem 6.7]. Ambro’s canonical bundle formula gives a complementary reduction: the lower-dimensional minimal model program and abundance settle a nef klt adjoint whose nef reduction has lower-dimensional image <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Ambro2005](#ref-Ambro2005), Theorem 4.3]. The nef reduction contracts the curves of degree zero through very general points; the dimension of its image is the *nef dimension*. This curve-theoretic dimension is distinct from numerical dimension, which is defined by nonzero intersection powers of the nef divisor.

The smooth theorem also has a log canonical consequence. Hashizume proved that smooth canonical nonvanishing in dimension $n$ implies nonvanishing for log canonical pairs with real boundaries, as well as log minimal models, in dimensions at most $n$ <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Hashizume2018](#ref-Hashizume2018), Theorem 1.4]. For rational data we replace the resulting effective real representative by a rational one, preserving its support and working on the original variety.

<a id="stmt-cor-lc-nonvanishing"></a>
<div id="cor:lc-nonvanishing" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a connected normal projective log canonical complex pair of dimension at most four. Suppose that $\Delta\geq0$ is rational and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef. For every positive integer $r$ such that $rD$ is Cartier, there is a positive integer $m$ with $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mrD))\ne0$.*

</div>

The corollary does not require $X$ to be $\mathbb Q$-factorial, and $m$ may depend on the pair and the prescribed index. Log abundance asks for semiampleness of the nef adjoint $K_X+\Delta$. The passage from the first section to that conclusion uses the supported-boundary lifting and abundance-after-nonvanishing theorems in the separate companion *Lifting sections from the reduced support of an adjoint <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAIBoundary2026](#ref-OpenAIBoundary2026), Theorems 1.1–1.2]* . Together with the corollary, they give fourfold log abundance; that consequence does not enter the proof below.

A recent comparison is Liu–Xu’s preprint on good minimal models for log canonical pairs of dimension at most five with nonnegative invariant Iitaka dimension and numerical dimension at most one <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LiuXu2025](#ref-LiuXu2025), Theorem 5.1]. Its hypotheses already include nonvanishing and restrict numerical dimension. The present smooth theorem obtains the first section without either restriction.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Geometry of a possible counterexample

Suppose nonvanishing fails. Existence of minimal models in dimension four allows us to fix a terminal minimal model $Y$, retaining the pluricanonical section spaces. The termination input used here is the ordinary-pair case of Chen–Tsakanikas <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas2023](#ref-ChenTsakanikas2023), Theorem 1.1]; it supplies a minimal model, without asserting semiampleness. Write $K=K_Y$. The divisor $K$ is nef and not big. The preceding reductions give $q(Y)=h^1(Y,\mathcal O_Y)=0$ and full nef dimension. On a fixed very general locus, every curve has positive $K$-degree and every proper positive-dimensional subvariety is of general type. A fixed Cartier index makes the positive curve degrees uniformly bounded away from zero.

Choose ample polarizations approaching the nef ray of $K$, scaled so that their volumes remain small while their degrees on curves through that locus tend to infinity. A section with unusually high vanishing at a moving point would force a persistent component in the base locus of the corresponding jet systems. Differentiation with respect to the moving point controls the multiplicity along that component. This uses the parameter-differentiation method of Ein–Küchle–Lazarsfeld <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995](#ref-EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995), Proposition 2.3]. We apply it to the whole space of sections satisfying the jet conditions, so the estimate controls ordinary powers of the ideal of the component. Intersection theory bounds its degree and canonical intersection. General type then produces a curve of bounded degree, contradicting the chosen scaling. The resulting upper bound applies to every section in every allowed Cartier degree. The independent moving-center estimates and the finite-cover lemma used in this argument and its two-factor extension are <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Lemmas 7.1–7.5]. We apply them to the fourfold geometry and prove the required curve and correspondence obstructions here.

A second use of this argument concerns sections over two copies of $Y$. Fix an integer $\iota>0$ making $S_0=\iota K$ Cartier and consider $$Z=\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal O_{Y\times Y}(S_{0,1})
             \oplus\mathcal O_{Y\times Y}(S_{0,2})\bigr)
       \longrightarrow Y\times Y.$$ A subscript indicates pullback from the corresponding factor. The two summands give two distinguished sections of this projective bundle. The symmetric-power formula records the distributions of a fixed tensor exponent between the two factors: its summands are $\mathcal O(aS_{0,1}+bS_{0,2})$, for nonnegative integers $a,b$ with $a+b$ fixed. A polarization on $Z$ combines the two base polarizations and a multiple of its tautological bundle. We show that it generates many jets at a very general point away from the two distinguished sections. Its Seshadri constant, the infimum of degree divided by multiplicity over curves through the point, measures this local positivity.

Moving base components in $Z$ have several possible images and fibers. One case requires an additional argument. Restrict to a slice where one base coordinate is fixed. A multisection different from the two distinguished sections meets them in divisors whose difference represents a multiple of $K$. This is a *signed representative*: its coefficients may be positive or negative. We must prove that the logarithmic adjoint on a resolution of its full support is big. This is where minimal metrics and ordinary cohomological injectivity enter the geometry.

The companion *Minimal metrics and interior injectivity for nef adjoints <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[OpenAIMetrics2026](#ref-OpenAIMetrics2026)]*  supplies two analytic inputs. Every minimal semipositive metric on the pullback of a nef projective klt adjoint has zero Lelong numbers. In addition, an injectivity theorem on ordinary $H^1$ compares an interior rational boundary with an endpoint boundary when the two endpoint bundles carry such metrics. The exact hypotheses are recorded in Section <a href="#sec:analytic-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic-inputs">4</a>. These statements concern actual rational line bundles; numerical equivalence would not suffice.

The cohomological method draws on the harmonic-form approach of Enoki, as extended by Fujino to singular metrics, and on Matsumura’s treatment of metrics with transcendental singularities <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[FujinoInjectivity2012](#ref-FujinoInjectivity2012), Theorem 1.2, Lemmas 3.1–3.2, and Section 3, Claim 1]<a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Matsumura2018](#ref-Matsumura2018), Theorems 1.3 and 5.9, Sections 5.2–5.3]. Fujino’s complete metrics on an analytic complement and his comparison with coherent cohomology with multiplier ideals give a model for the setup. Matsumura supplies uniform estimates for primitives of exact forms.

Applied to two nearby nef klt adjoints, the cohomological injection makes restriction to a whole reduced non-klt boundary surjective. Lower-dimensional abundance and gluing across the conductor supply a section on that boundary. Once this section has been lifted, Gongyo–Matsumura’s fourfold criterion converts nonvanishing and the zero-Lelong metric into semiampleness <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GongyoMatsumura2017](#ref-GongyoMatsumura2017), Corollary 5.3]. This order matters: the criterion is applied after the first section has been obtained. The signed-representative argument then excludes the offending multisection. The remaining analysis also excludes dominant finite correspondences, using a fixed branch complement and the positive canonical degree of curves.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From jets to a rank contradiction

We now have opposing estimates. Sections on one copy of $Y$ can vanish only to a small order, while the projective bundle over $Y\times Y$ has enough jets to give a large evaluation rank. We fix the polarization, a smooth resolution and a flag of general hyperplane sections ending in a curve, together with one finite system of jets. The scalar estimate also supplies a fixed movable curve on the blowup of a point. Its intersection with effective divisors will bound orders of sections after reduction. We spread all these finite data and reduce modulo sufficiently large primes.

Ordinary jets produce an evaluation matrix whose generic rank is large. On the diagonal, the canonical Frobenius filtration expresses its possible values through truncated powers of the cotangent bundle. A single flag estimate bounds the sum of the dimensions of these spaces, forcing a much smaller rank there. A nonzero maximal determinant must therefore vanish to high order at a suitable diagonal point. Its two actual determinant line bundles, one from each factor, have classes controlled by the fixed movable curve. This bounds the order of every section of either factor on the reduction itself. The resulting small determinant order is incompatible with the order forced by the rank drop.

The filtration has a substantial history. For curves, the diagonal filtration appears in Raynaud’s work <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Raynaud1982](#ref-Raynaud1982), Remarques 4.1.2(2)]; Joshi–Ramanan–Xia–Yu formulate it using the Cartier connection <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[JoshiRamananXiaYu2006](#ref-JoshiRamananXiaYu2006), Section 5.3]. Sun and Kitadai–Sumihiro give the higher-dimensional filtration and its local description by truncated monomials <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Sun2008](#ref-Sun2008), Theorem 3.7]<a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KitadaiSumihiro2008](#ref-KitadaiSumihiro2008), Corollary 3.5]. Mustaţă–Schwede’s comparison of ordinary and Frobenius powers provides the local jet conversion <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[MustataSchwede2014](#ref-MustataSchwede2014), proof of Proposition 2.12, Equation (2.8)], and Langer’s estimate controls the Frobenius instability error <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Langer2004](#ref-Langer2004), Corollary 2.5]. The argument combines these tools with the two-factor geometry and the common flag estimate. Section <a href="#sec:frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frobenius">7</a> specifies the numerical separation between the resulting ranks and orders and verifies the inputs of the common theorem.

The complete finite-data Frobenius comparison is proved independently in <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Theorem 9.1]. It is also applicable to the all-dimensional abundance argument. Its hypotheses are the stated geometric data and numerical inequalities. No all-dimensional abundance conclusion or logarithmic Iitaka subadditivity is used in the fourfold preparation of these data.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Reading order.

Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> fixes the terminal model and its curve-degree obstruction. Section <a href="#sec:moving-centers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:moving-centers">3</a> develops the moving-center tools and proves the scalar order bound. Section <a href="#sec:analytic-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic-inputs">4</a> states the analytic inputs, and Section <a href="#sec:signed-representatives" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signed-representatives">5</a> proves logarithmic general type for signed supports. Section <a href="#sec:two-slot-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:two-slot-jets">6</a> excludes the product’s moving centers and obtains the two-slot jets. Section <a href="#sec:frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frobenius">7</a> verifies the finite data for the common Frobenius theorem, completing smooth nonvanishing. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">8</a> gives the original-variety and prescribed-index consequence.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Conventions.

Varieties are over $\mathbb C$ except when the finite-data comparison is reduced to positive characteristic. A subvariety is of general type when a smooth projective resolution is of general type. Rational line bundles and their sections are interpreted after clearing specified denominators. An inequality in nef order means that its difference is nef. A very general locus is the complement of a countable union of proper closed subsets. The symbols $r$ for a prescribed Cartier index, $r_t$ for a scaling integer, $q(Y)$ for irregularity, and $q$ for a projective-bundle weight have distinct uses.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### A fixed minimal model of full nef dimension

Assume that smooth canonical nonvanishing fails in dimension four. We first choose a terminal minimal model on which every curve through a very general point has a fixed positive lower bound for its canonical degree. We then show that every proper positive-dimensional subvariety through such a point is of general type. These two properties will control the moving centers of the later jet argument. The model remains fixed while its ample perturbations vary.

<a id="stmt-prop-reduction"></a>
<div id="prop:reduction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3**. *If [thm:nonvanishing](#thm:nonvanishing) fails, there is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold $Y$, with $K=K_Y$, such that*

1.  *$K$ is nef, $\kappa(Y,K)=-\infty$, and $K^4=0$;*

2.  *$q(Y)=0$ and the nef dimension of $K$ is four;*

3.  *for some integer $\iota>0$, $\iota K$ is Cartier and <a id="eq:curve-lower-bound"></a>$$\label{eq:curve-lower-bound}
     K\cdot C\ge \frac1\iota$$ for every integral curve $C$ through a point of a fixed very general locus in $Y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* Start with a smooth counterexample $H$. Run the canonical minimal model program. Existence of the necessary flips follows from <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010), Corollary 1.4.1]. Termination in the pseudo-effective fourfold case is <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas2023](#ref-ChenTsakanikas2023), Theorem 1.1], applied with zero nef data to the ordinary pair with zero boundary. Divisorial contractions lower the Picard number, so together these statements give termination of the program. Pseudo-effectivity is preserved and excludes a Mori fiber space as its endpoint. We obtain a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal minimal model $Y$.

Canonical section spaces in sufficiently divisible degrees are birationally invariant here. On a common resolution the differences from the respective pulled-back canonical divisors are effective exceptional divisors, and pushing their sheaves forward does not add sections. Thus $\kappa(Y,K)=-\infty$. In particular $K$ is not big; as it is nef, this says $K^4=0$.

Terminal singularities are rational. Hence $q(Y)$ agrees with the irregularity of a resolution. If it were positive, <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Fujino2010](#ref-Fujino2010), Corollary 4.7] would make $K$ semiample, a contradiction. Likewise, if the nef dimension were at most three, <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Ambro2005](#ref-Ambro2005), Theorem 4.3], with the established log minimal model program and log abundance in those dimensions, would make $K$ semiample. This includes nef dimension zero. Thus the nef dimension is four.

The curve property of nef reduction <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[BauerEtAl2002](#ref-BauerEtAl2002), Theorem 2.1] says in this case that $K\cdot C>0$ for every integral curve through a very general point. Choose $\iota$ with $\iota K$ Cartier. Its degree on an integral complete curve is an integer, so positivity gives [eq:curve-lower-bound](#eq:curve-lower-bound). This uses only the curve property of nef reduction, not an Iitaka fibration on $Y$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-subvarieties"></a>
<div id="prop:subvarieties" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4**. *For $Y$ as in , every proper positive-dimensional subvariety through a point of a suitable fixed very general locus is of general type.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-subvarieties"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-subvarieties</summary>

*Proof.* We first work with one family of subvarieties, and at the end make the very general locus independent of the family. Hilbert schemes give countably many finite-type parameter spaces for integral subvarieties of $Y$. Stratification, resolution of the universal families, and generic smoothness give countably many smooth families $$b:\mathcal V\longrightarrow B,\qquad e:\mathcal V\longrightarrow Y,$$ whose fibers are smooth projective resolutions of their images and whose fiber maps are birational onto those images. To obtain this description, resolve the total universal family on an integral parameter stratum, shrink until its fibers are smooth and the fiber maps birational, and repeat on the complement by noetherian induction. Non-dominant evaluation families can be discarded by excluding their image closures in $Y$.

Consider a dominant family with fibers of dimension $d$, where $0<d<4$. We may slice the parameter space to dimension $4-d$ so that $e$ becomes generically finite and remains dominant. Indeed, the parameters incident to a general point of $Y$ have a component of dimension $\dim B-(4-d)$ on the locus where the fiber maps are generically embeddings. General ample cuts in a compactification of $B$ meet this locus in finitely many points. We take the cuts very generally, so a very general point of the slice has the generic value of every plurigenus of the original family. There are only countably many such conditions.

On the resulting smooth total space, ramification and terminality give $$K_{\mathcal V}\sim_\mathbb Qe^*K+E_{\mathcal V},\qquad E_{\mathcal V}\ge0.$$ For completeness, the rational lift of $e$ to a resolution of $Y$ is defined at codimension-one points by properness. The usual ramification formula there, followed by the effective discrepancies of the terminal target, proves this divisor inequality. Its restriction to a very general fiber is <a id="eq:fiber-canonical-comparison"></a>$$\label{eq:fiber-canonical-comparison}
 K_V\sim_\mathbb Qe_V^*K+E_V,\qquad E_V\ge0.$$ In particular $K_V$ is pseudo-effective.

The dimension of $V$ is at most three. The minimal model program and abundance in these dimensions give a good terminal minimal model $V_{\min}$. Suppose that $V$ is not of general type. The semiample canonical divisor of $V_{\min}$ defines a morphism with connected positive-dimensional general fibers, on each of which a multiple of $K_{V_{\min}}$ is trivial. On a common resolution $\widetilde V$ of $V$ and $V_{\min}$, there is a complete curve through a very general point with $K_{\widetilde V}$-degree zero. Here is the avoidance needed for this assertion. The singular locus of the terminal model and the centers of the exceptional divisors have codimension at least two. A general fiber either misses a given vertical center or meets a dominant center in codimension at least two. General sufficiently ample complete intersections in that fiber through a prescribed very general point give a curve avoiding all such centers; for a one-dimensional fiber the fiber itself does so. Its strict transform has canonical degree zero by the exceptional discrepancy formula.

Pulling back [eq:fiber-canonical-comparison](#eq:fiber-canonical-comparison) to $\widetilde V$ and adding the effective discrepancies of $\widetilde V\to V$ gives $$K_{\widetilde V}\sim_\mathbb Q\widetilde e^{\,*}K+\widetilde E,
 \qquad \widetilde E\ge0.$$ Choose the preceding curve through a point outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\widetilde E$ and outside the exceptional locus of the birational map onto the image subvariety. It is not contained in $\widetilde E$, and its image in $Y$ is a curve. Thus its pulled-back $K$-degree is at most zero. Dominance of the total evaluation lets us choose the point in the very general locus of [eq:curve-lower-bound](#eq:curve-lower-bound), which gives a contradiction.

We have proved that the very general fiber of the sliced family is of general type. By the choice of slice its plurigenera are the generic plurigenera of the original smooth family. Upper semicontinuity shows that all fibers of that family have at least these plurigenera, hence are of general type as well. Finally exclude the image closures of all non-dominant families. Countability of the parameter strata gives the asserted very general locus. ◻

</details>

For the rest of the contradiction argument, fix $Y$, $K$, $\iota$, and a very general locus on which both propositions apply. We next choose ample perturbations of $K$ with bounded volume and growing degree on every curve through this locus. The two propositions will then turn a section of excessive vanishing order into a contradiction.

<a id="sec:moving-centers"></a>
### Moving centers and scalar vanishing

We work on the terminal fourfold $Y$ from [sec:reduction](#sec:reduction), write $K=K_Y$, and put $n=4$. Write $Y^{\mathrm{vg}}$ for a fixed very general locus on which both apply. Thus every curve through this locus has $K$-degree at least $1/\iota$, and every proper positive-dimensional subvariety through it is of general type. We will bound the order of every section of every Cartier multiple of a suitable ample rational divisor at a very general point. Fix a very ample Cartier divisor $A$ on $Y$. For a fixed positive rational $\epsilon$ and positive rational $t$ tending to zero, define <a id="eq:small-volume-polarization"></a>$$\label{eq:small-volume-polarization}
 \mu_t=\bigl((K+tA)^n\bigr)^{1/n},\qquad
 r_t\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\quad r_t\mu_t\longrightarrow\epsilon,
 \qquad L=L_t=r_t(K+tA).$$ Such integers $r_t$ exist, for example by rounding $\epsilon/\mu_t$. Since $K$ is nef and not big, $\mu_t>0$ tends to zero. Hence <a id="eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound"></a>$$\label{eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound}
 r_t\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad
 L_t^n\longrightarrow\epsilon^n,
 \qquad L_t\cdot C\geq r_t/\iota$$ for every integral curve $C$ through that very general locus. The integers $r_t$ are unrelated to the original Cartier index $r$ in .

A section with excessive vanishing will yield a curve through $Y^{\mathrm{vg}}$ whose $L_t$-degree is bounded independently of $t$, contradicting [eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound](#eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound). To construct that curve, we follow the base loci of the full jet kernels as their marked point moves. A component that persists at two successive jet levels has high multiplicity; this bounds both its degree and its canonical intersection. General type then produces a curve of bounded degree. We first give the three moving-center estimates from <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Lemmas 7.1–7.4], including the differentiation argument responsible for ordinary ideal powers. These inputs are independent of abundance and subadditivity. We then prove the curve obstruction and apply it to scalar vanishing.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Numerical subadjunction at a generic lc center

The numerical statement below only requires log canonicity near the generic point of the center. This is the form needed for boundaries obtained from a linear system.

The subadjunction method relates an lc center to the canonical bundle formula. Kawamata’s minimal-center theorem and the later unperturbed form of Fujino–Gongyo are important antecedents <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[KawamataSubadjunction1998](#ref-KawamataSubadjunction1998), [FujinoGongyoSubadjunction2012](#ref-FujinoGongyoSubadjunction2012)]. The numerical form below permits a center that is only generically lc and need not be minimal. Its proof in the companion uses a dlt stratum, the generic rank-one condition, b-nef moduli, and finite-base ramification; it is not an application of those antecedents with their hypotheses omitted.

<a id="stmt-lem-numerical-subadjunction"></a>
<div id="lem:numerical-subadjunction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Numerical generic subadjunction). *Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety over $\mathbb C$, let $\Theta\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety. Suppose that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $Z$, and let $\rho:V^*\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then <a id="eq:numerical-subadjunction"></a>$$\label{eq:numerical-subadjunction}
 K_{V^*}\cdot(\rho^*P)^{f-1}
 \leq (K_Z+\Theta)|_V\cdot(P|_V)^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection of the restricted rational Cartier class with the cycle $[V]$. No $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.*

</div>

The proof is given in the independent moving-center part of <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Lemma 7.1]<a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Lemma 7.2].

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Degree and canonical bounds for a base component

The following companion lemma applies numerical subadjunction to a boundary built from an actual linear system. Its local multiplicity controls both the degree of the center and the coefficient of that boundary.

<a id="stmt-lem-base-component"></a>
<div id="lem:base-component" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (A base component of high multiplicity). *Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\ne\mathcal H\subset H^0(Z,\mathcal O_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\eta_V$, and that $\eta_V\in Z_{\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\mathfrak m=\mathfrak m_{\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}}$. If $$\mathfrak b\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}\subset\mathfrak m^h
 \qquad(h\in\mathbb Z_{>0}),$$ then <a id="eq:base-component-degree"></a>$$\label{eq:base-component-degree}
 P^f\cdot V\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ If $f>0$, there is a rational number $c$ with $0<c\leq ak/h$ such that, on a smooth resolution $V^*$, <a id="eq:base-component-canonical"></a>$$\label{eq:base-component-canonical}
 K_{V^*}\cdot P^{f-1}
 \leq (K_Z+cP)|_V\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The coefficient $c$ may be chosen with an effective rational divisor $\Theta\sim_{\mathbb Q}cP$ such that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. Thus can also be applied with a different nef testing class. The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.*

</div>

The proof is given in the independent moving-center part of .

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Differentiating the full moving jet kernel

We use the parameter-differentiation method developed in <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995](#ref-EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995), Proposition 2.3]. The form used here retains ordinary ideal powers and isolated base components. Fixing a high order at a varying point produces a family of linear subspaces of one fixed section space. A component common to two successive base loci acquires high multiplicity, because parameter derivatives of the higher kernel still belong to the lower kernel. We state both the parameter-family conclusion and the local slicing property that will be used later.

<a id="stmt-lem-moving-multiplicity"></a>
<div id="lem:moving-multiplicity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Moving multiplicity). *Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\tau_0<\tau_1<\cdots<\tau_d,
 \qquad\tau_{i+1}-\tau_i=\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\ceil{k\tau_i}$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\delta\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\Gamma=\{(t,z):z\in V_t\}\subset T\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in <a id="eq:moving-multiplicity-order"></a>$$\label{eq:moving-multiplicity-order}
 \mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\qquad h=\ceil{k\delta/2},
 \qquad k/h\leq2/\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*

*More precisely, the incidence base ideal lies in $\mathcal I_\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\Gamma$. If a further morphism $Z\to B$ is given, one may choose a general incidence point where the base support is only $V_t$ and $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its image in $B$. For the fiber component $F$ through that point, assume that $F$ is proper in the integral ambient fiber component under consideration. The restricted series is then nonzero and has base ideal in the ordinary $\mathcal I_F^h$ and has $F$ as an isolated base component there. This last assertion concerns the scheme fiber locally at that chosen point, and does not assert transversality of every fiber. Smoothness near the generic point of $F$, and the projectivity and klt hypotheses needed for on the ambient fiber, must be checked in each application.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-moving-multiplicity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-moving-multiplicity</summary>

*Proof.* We recall the argument of <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAIAbundance2026), Lemmas 7.3–7.4] to explain why the estimate concerns the entire kernel and ordinary ideal powers. On the open where the jet maps have constant rank, their kernels are vector bundles with fibers $\mathcal H_i(x)$. Their base loci increase with $i$. Starting with a positive-dimensional component through the mark, follow a containing component at each level. The $d+1$ components all have dimensions in $[1,d-1]$, so two successive ones coincide. After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, their geometric generic components and inclusions spread to one family $\Gamma\subset T\times Z$. Its evaluation dominates $Z$, since each member contains its moving mark $x(t)$.

Take a local frame of the higher kernel and trivialize $kP$ near $z$ with a frame independent of $t$. In a fixed basis of the global section space, each frame section has the form $$s(t,z)=\sum_\alpha c_\alpha(t)s_\alpha(z).$$ Differentiation in the parameter with $z$ fixed therefore gives another global section of $kP$. A derivative of order $r$ lowers the vanishing order at $x(t)$ by at most $r$. It belongs to the entire lower kernel whenever $$r\leq\ceil{k\tau_{i+1}}-\ceil{k\tau_i}.$$ The right side is at least $k\delta-1$, so every derivative of order less than $h=\ceil{k\delta/2}$ belongs to that kernel and vanishes on $\Gamma$. This is where using the full kernels matters: an arbitrarily chosen subseries need not contain these derivatives.

To turn these vanishings into ordinary ideal membership, work at a general smooth incidence point. Since $\Gamma\to Z$ is submersive, parameter directions span its normal space in $T\times Z$. Choose parameter coordinates $t=(t',t'')$ so that the implicit function theorem writes $\Gamma$ as $t'=F(t'',z)$. With $t''$ and $z$ fixed, derivatives in $t'$ are exactly derivatives in the normal coordinates $u=t'-F(t'',z)$. All normal Taylor coefficients of degree below $h$ therefore vanish, proving $s\in\mathcal I_\Gamma^h$. Faithful flatness of analytic local rings over algebraic local rings gives the same membership algebraically. There are finitely many frame sections, so this containment holds for the entire incidence base ideal on a dense algebraic open.

Generic smoothness of $\Gamma\to T$ identifies its scheme fiber there with the reduced member $V_t$. Restriction gives $\mathcal I_\Gamma^h\mathcal O_{\{t\}\times Z}=\mathcal I_{V_t}^h$, and the frame specializes to a basis of the entire higher kernel. Isolation holds because $V_t$ is a base-locus component. Finally remove all other base components and choose the incidence point where $V_t$ is smooth over its actual image in $B$. Its scheme fiber is then reduced locally, so restriction to the ambient fiber sends $\mathcal I_{V_t}^h$ to $\mathcal I_F^h$ and the base support to $F$. If $F$ is proper in the chosen integral ambient fiber component, that support is proper there; hence the restricted series is nonzero and has $F$ as an isolated base component at its generic point. ◻

</details>

For a fixed gap $\delta$, combining gives degree and canonical-intersection bounds for a repeated moving component, with coefficients independent of the large divisible integer $k$. The next lemma converts such intersection bounds into a curve of bounded degree. Its general-type hypothesis supplies a birational linear system in a degree depending only on the dimension. The scalar application uses and has no boundary. We include a reduced boundary in the statement because the two-slot argument will also need its logarithmic form.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### A uniform obstruction from logarithmic general type

<a id="stmt-lem-degree-obstruction"></a>
<div id="lem:degree-obstruction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Bounded-degree curve obstruction). *Keep $Y$, $L_t$, and $\iota$ as in [eq:small-volume-polarization](#eq:small-volume-polarization), [eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound](#eq:small-volume-curve-lower-bound). Fix $f\geq1$ and positive constants $C_0,C_1$. Suppose, for arbitrarily small $t$, that there are a smooth projective variety $V_t^*$ of dimension $f$, a reduced simple normal crossings divisor $G_t$ on it, a nef rational divisor $P_t$, and a morphism $e_t:V_t^*\to Y$ satisfying the following conditions:*

1.  *$K_{V_t^*}+G_t$ is big;*

2.  *$0<P_t^f\leq C_0$ and $(K_{V_t^*}+G_t)\cdot P_t^{f-1}\leq C_1P_t^f$;*

3.  *$e_t$ is generically finite onto its positive-dimensional image, and that image meets the fixed very general locus of $Y$;*

4.  *$P_t-e_t^*L_t$ is nef.*

*Then these conditions are impossible for all sufficiently small $t$. The case $G_t=0$ includes ordinary general type.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-degree-obstruction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-degree-obstruction</summary>

*Proof.* Uniform effective birationality for projective lc pairs of dimension $f$ with big adjoint and coefficients in the fixed DCC set $\{0,1\}$ supplies an integer $b=b(f)$ such that $|b(K_{V_t^*}+G_t)|$ is birational <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[HMX2018](#ref-HMX2018), Theorem 4.0.1]. Resolve its base ideal and denote the free moving divisor by $H_b$. We keep the same notation for the pullbacks of $P_t$ and $L_t$. The fixed divisor is effective, so $$H_b\cdot P_t^{f-1}
 \leq b(K_{V_t^*}+G_t)\cdot P_t^{f-1}
