For relative Frobenius $F:W\to W'$, let $S_0'$ be the base-field twist of $S_0$ and put $$\mathcal E=F_*\mathcal O_W(B_p),\qquad
 s=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\mathcal E=p^n,\qquad R=s^2=p^8,\qquad
 U_a=H^0(W,\mathcal O_W(B_p+paS_0)).$$ The projection formula gives an evaluation map $$\label{eq:finite-evaluation}
 \Phi_p:\bigoplus_{\substack{a+b=q\\a,b\ge0}}
 U_a\otimes U_b\otimes\mathcal O_{W'\times W'}(-aS'_{0,1}-bS'_{0,2})
 \longrightarrow\mathcal E\boxtimes\mathcal E.$$ Products of the fixed jet sections, multiplied by the fixed fillers, generate the local quotient by the $p$-th powers of the parameters at $z_*$. Let $A_*$ be the tensor product of the two base Frobenius-fiber algebras. If the torus coordinate $z$ has value $z_0\ne0$ at $z_*$, this quotient is $A_*[z]/(z^p-z_0^p)$. Project to the coefficient of $1$ in its $A_*$-basis $1,z,\ldots,z^{p-1}$. Exactly the weights divisible by $p$ survive, showing that the values of <a href="#eq:finite-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-evaluation">[eq:finite-evaluation]</a> span $A_*$. Its dimension is $R$, so $\Phi_p$ has generic rank $R$.

On the diagonal, the columns instead restrict to global sections of one line bundle on the Frobenius fiber product: $$\Delta_F=W\times_{W'}W,\qquad
 \mathcal L_p=
 \mathcal O_{\Delta_F}(B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}+pqS_{0,1}).$$ Here $pS_{0,1}$ and $pS_{0,2}$ are isomorphic line bundles on $\Delta_F$, so all the weight pairs $a+b=q$ give this same bundle. The rank at every diagonal point is at most $h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p)$. The diagonal-ideal filtration has graded bundles $$\mathcal G_j=\mathcal O_W(2B_p+pqS_0)\otimes A_j(\Omega_W^1),
 \qquad 0\le j\le n(p-1),$$ where $A_j$ is the degree-$j$ part of the symmetric algebra modulo the $p$-th powers of its local generators. If $e_j=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}A_j$, then $\sum_j e_j=p^n$. The slope estimate and our one fixed flag give the common bound proved in the companion: $$h^0(W,\mathcal G_j)
 \le e_jp^n\bigl(7^nH^n+O(p^{-1})\bigr).$$ The error is independent of $j$. Summing therefore bounds every diagonal rank by $R(7^nH^n+O(p^{-1}))<R/4$, since $7^nH^n\le(14\epsilon)^n<1/4$.

Choose $R$ generically independent columns of $\Phi_p$, and let $m_i$ be the sum of their weights in slot $i$. Their nonzero determinant is a section $$0\ne\sigma\in H^0(W'\times W',\mathcal Q_1\boxtimes\mathcal Q_2),
 \qquad
 \mathcal Q_i=(\det\mathcal E)^{\otimes s}
                 \otimes\mathcal O_{W'}(m_iS_0').$$ These are actual line bundles. Under the numerical identification with the base-field twist, their first Chern classes satisfy <a id="eq:finite-determinant-classes"></a>$$\label{eq:finite-determinant-classes}
 \frac{c_1(\mathcal Q_i)}R
 =\frac{B_p}p+\frac{p-1}{2p}K_W+\lambda_iS_0
 =Q_{\lambda_i}+\frac{B_p-pL-K_W/2}{p},
 \qquad \lambda_i=\frac{m_i}{R}\in[0,q].$$ The numerator of the last term belongs to a fixed finite list. Thus the endpoint comparison bounds the intersection of each normalized class with $\gamma$ by $(40\epsilon+O(p^{-1}))J_x\gamma$, uniformly in the chosen columns.

This intersection bound controls sections on the reduction itself. Put $x'=F(x)$. For any nonzero section of $\mathcal Q_i$, the strict transform of its zero divisor on the blowup at $x'$ is effective and pairs nonnegatively with the twist of $\gamma$: that curve is still the pushforward of the fixed ample complete intersection. Since $J_x\gamma>0$, every such section has order at $x'$ at most $R(40\epsilon+O(p^{-1}))$. Bases adapted to vanishing order in the two factors show that a nonzero section of their external product has order at most the sum of these bounds. Within each bidegree the leading tensors are linearly independent, and different bidegrees cannot cancel. Consequently $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\le R(80\epsilon+O(p^{-1})).$$ But the rank of $\Phi_p$ at $(x',x')$ is less than $R/4$. Constant row operations make more than $3R/4$ rows of the selected matrix vanish at that point, forcing $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\ge3R/4$. The two orders contradict $80\epsilon<3/4$ for large $p$. The generic-rank calculation used $z_*$, whereas this diagonal comparison uses the independently chosen point $x$.

Thus the terminal counterexample in cannot exist. Canonical section spaces are preserved by its birational construction, so this proves [thm:nonvanishing](#thm:nonvanishing).

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### A section on the original normal variety

The geometric argument produces a pluricanonical section on a smooth fourfold. Hashizume’s reduction gives an effective real representative of an lc adjoint on the original variety. The following elementary lemma turns that representative into a rational one. It concerns finite linear algebra and uses no nonvanishing or abundance theorem.

<a id="stmt-lem-rational-feasibility"></a>
<div id="lem:rational-feasibility" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:rational-feasibility"></a>
**Lemma 20** (Finite rational feasibility). *Let $M=(m_{ij})\in\operatorname{Mat}_{w\times v}(\mathbb Q)$ and $d\in\mathbb Q^w$. If the system $$\label{eq:rational-feasibility}
 d_i-y_i=\sum_{j=1}^{v}m_{ij}b_j\quad(1\le i\le w),
 \qquad y_i\ge0$$ has a real solution, it has a rational solution. Moreover, one may require precisely the same $y_i$ to be zero as in the given solution. Consequently, if $D$ is a rational Weil divisor on a normal integral variety $X$ over a field $k$ and $D\sim_{\mathbb R}G$ for an effective real Weil divisor $G$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}G_{\mathbb Q}$ for an effective rational divisor with $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G_{\mathbb Q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G$. If $rD$ is Cartier for a prescribed positive integer $r$, then $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mrD))\ne0$ for some $m>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-rational-feasibility"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-rational-feasibility</summary>

*Proof.* Fix the equations $y_i=0$ at all coordinates vanishing in the given real solution. Together with the displayed equations they define a nonempty affine space over $\mathbb Q$. Gaussian elimination over $\mathbb Q$ gives a rational particular solution and a basis over $\mathbb Q$ for its direction space. Thus its rational points are dense in its real points. Approximate the given point closely enough to keep all of the finitely many remaining coordinates $y_i$ strictly positive. This proves the first assertion, including the case where all $y_i$ vanish or the affine space has dimension zero.

For the divisor assertion, choose a finite expression $$D-G=\sum_{j=1}^{v} a_j\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(f_j),\qquad
 a_j\in\mathbb R,\quad f_j\in k(X)^*.$$ List the prime divisors in the supports of $D,G$, and these finitely many principal divisors as $P_1,\ldots,P_w$. Write $D=\sum d_iP_i$ and let $m_{ij}\in\mathbb Z$ be the coefficient of $P_i$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(f_j)$. The coefficients of $G$ and the $a_j$ give a real solution of <a href="#eq:rational-feasibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rational-feasibility">[eq:rational-feasibility]</a>. A rational solution gives $G_{\mathbb Q}=\sum y_iP_i\ge0$ with the same support and $D-G_{\mathbb Q}=\sum b_j\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(f_j)$.

Choose a positive integer $N$ divisible by $r$ and by the denominators of all $y_i,b_j$. Then $$ND-NG_{\mathbb Q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}\!\left(\prod_j f_j^{Nb_j}\right).$$ The effective integral divisor $NG_{\mathbb Q}$ is Cartier, since $ND$ is Cartier and their difference is principal. It defines a nonzero section of $\mathcal O_X(ND)$. Write $N=mr$. No $\mathbb Q$-factoriality is needed: all equalities were equalities of Weil divisors on $X$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-rational-feasibility-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-rational-feasibility</summary>

*Proof of .* Hashizume’s reduction <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[Hashizume2018](#ref-Hashizume2018), Theorem 1.4] takes smooth canonical nonvanishing in dimension four, supplied by [thm:nonvanishing](#thm:nonvanishing), and gives nonvanishing for projective lc pairs with effective real boundaries and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoints in dimensions at most four. Applied to the given rational lc pair, it produces $D\sim_{\mathbb R}G\ge0$ on the original normal variety; nefness implies pseudo-effectivity. gives a nonzero section of $mrD$ for some positive integer $m$, with the prescribed index $r$. ◻

</details>

{

      "source_pm_id": "PM-034.6",
      "source_quote": "Let $(H,\\Theta)$ be a normal connected projective complex klt pair, where $\\Theta\\ge0$ is rational and $D_H=K_H+\\Theta$ is nef and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier.",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-adjoint-metric",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-034.5",
      "target_quote": "Let $(H,\\Theta)$ be a normal connected projective complex klt pair, with $\\Theta$ an effective rational divisor. Suppose that $D_H=K_H+\\Theta$ is a nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",

      "relation": "specialises",
      "source_anchor": "thm:nonvanishing",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-034.6",
      "source_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex fourfold. If $K_X$ is pseudo-effective, then $H^0(X,mK_X)\\ne0$ for some positive integer $m$.",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-canonical-good-models",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-034.8",
      "target_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_T$.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The nonvanishing theorem provides the necessary sections and nonnegative Kodaira dimension assumed by the fourfold abundance criterion.",
      "relation": "prerequisite",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-nonvanishing",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-034.6",
      "source_quote": "there is a positive integer $m$ with $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(mrD))\\ne0$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing",
